Owner report 2026-07-23: the published documentation site showed stale history / CI status / git commits. Root causes: (a) documentation.yml publishes only on master pushes (last: June 17) and BOTH June-17 runs died in the Pages deploy ('git remote rm origin' exit 128 under force_orphan); (b) the site's _commits.json was a June-12 one-off snapshot with no refresh step; (c) no status data existed at all. Site pipeline: - NEW docs/site/_refresh_data.py: regenerates _commits.json (300 commits) + _status.json (fw/ULP/hub versions, requirements registry, CI snapshot via gh, latest commit) from the live repo. - _build_site.py renders a Project-status card on the index (versions, registry, per-workflow CI badges, snapshot stamp + live Actions link). - documentation.yml: publishes from dev AND master + workflow_dispatch; fetch-depth 0; refresh step before mkdocs; force_orphan dropped (the June-17 failure); permissions narrowed to read. fw 0.1.135: #53 storage fix — bootstat on raw RTC slow-mem words

2026-07-23 338ad3d4
fw 0.1.131: deque stamp fix — wrap clamp, rate hygiene, seeded rate (#51)
Audit verdict (70/70 crossings registered, stamps corrupt in 3 modes): - Mode 2: half-range clamp (delta > 0x80000000 -> stamp at poll time). Closure: observed 105,703,256 ms == 2^32/40.617 exactly. - Mode 3: calibration moved after the batch, gated on clamp-free, reference reset on clamp (kills the undershoot AND the stored-ref overshoot echo); band tightened 30-400 -> 110-170 ticks/ms (RC_SLOW nominal 136 +/-25%). - Mode 1: rate seeds at 136.0 so first post-boot/recovery batch back-dates within clock tolerance; -1 sentinel retired. DEQUE_AUDIT batch events carry the per-batch clamped count. No ULP change; SWR-289 contract unchanged; verify 139 PASS / 0 FAIL. Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

ULP TestOp dev.0.6: #48 defense final shape — tick-top only (dev.0.3 profile)
Every variant calling DEFEND_CLOCK from the deep chain was stack-unsafe: dev.0.4 (per iteration) tripped 5/5 overnight; dev.0.5 (window entries + write sites) tripped intermittently and wedged two sessions at sample 0 (pump-dead ULP + the SWR-258 AC-first wait hangs the CPU silently — unbounded-wait bug filed separately). Mechanism: a call added to leaf-optimized functions forces +16 B frames at max depth, invisible to the static headroom number. dev.0.6 = tick-top call only, 556 B headroom, the accepted mid-window residual documented in #48. 2026-07-22 0b8f3417
ULP TestOp dev.0.3: #48 FIX — ULP defends its own RTC pad register clock
SET_PERI_REG_MASK(SENS_SAR_PERI_CLK_GATE_CONF_REG, SENS_IOMUX_CLK_EN) at the top of every 1 ms tick: any CPU-side gating (machine.Pin / rtc_gpio_deinit clears the clock GLOBALLY for all 22 RTC pads) now heals within one tick. Conviction: dev.0.2 SNAP 0xa0000000 — clock read 0 at the failed kick, measured by the ULP at the failure instant. Pump-pad ownership enforced at silicon level (owner principle: pumps entirely under ULP control). Capture word stays armed for the trials; pump-identity bit dropped to hold headroom at 524 B (fail line 512). Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
2026-07-22 dd1223dc
Merge branch 'rebuild/v1.25-int-wdt' into dev
2026-07-22 9a67a64f
ULP TestOp dev.0.2: #48 clock-gate conviction probe
Workflow-verified mechanism: SENS.sar_peri_clk_gate_conf.iomux_clk_en (0x60008904 bit31) is the register clock SHARED by all 22 RTC pads; IDF's rtcio_ll_function_select clears it GLOBALLY when any pin routes to digital (machine.Pin -> rtc_gpio_deinit), and the next lightsleep's EXT1 prepare re-enables it (= the self-healing). Gated clock = every ULP pad write silently lost. dev.0.2 latches ONE word at the first failed kick: 0xA0|pump + bit3 = SENS_IOMUX_CLK_EN at that instant (0 = GATED = conviction). dev.0.1's pad-readback + counter dropped: headroom 524 B (fail line 512; dev.0.1 sat at 480 and canary-tripped, blocking session resume). 2026-07-20 a96f1666
ULP 1.15.0: gate the hall wake to BP_RUNNING (counting unchanged) — #46 A/B
Version-diff of the aborted 40 h run against the 44 h reference found the prime suspect: ULP 1.13.0's centralized bp_hall_edge_service() calls wakeup_main_cpu() UNCONDITIONALLY from four sites — BP_RUNNING plus the dose/park drive windows, the AC busy slice and BP_PARKING entry. In 1.12.0 the BP hall wake existed ONLY inside BP_RUNNING. So since 1.13.0 every dose pokes the main CPU while it is awake (dosing polls on plain asyncio), leaving the RTC SW-CPU interrupt latched for the next machine.lightsleep() to enter against — precisely where #46 kills the board (breadcrumb 22), and precisely when the resets cluster. Rates: 0 resets / 22 sample-ends on fw 0.1.114 + ULP 1.12.0, versus 3 / 10 on fw 0.1.125 + ULP 1.13.0. Fisher exact p~0.02 — a real regression, not chance. Fix keeps what SWR-289 actually needed: crossings are still counted and timestamped in EVERY state (the multi-spot ledger depends on that), only the wake is gated. Nothing in the firmware depends on a mid-dose wake. Deliberately one variable: firmware stays 0.1.125; the ULP deploy carries the regenerated map (binary 7268 B, headroom 568 B LOW-accepted, canary armed). 1.14.0 is burned by the withdrawn breadcrumb build. Registry DOC 1.87; verify 160 reqs, 139 PASS / 0 FAIL. Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-07-20 20860ef1
SWR-294: transactional, import-proven firmware deploy (rollback bench-proven)
A deploy can no longer leave the board unbootable. Files stage as <dest>.new and are length+FNV verified before ANY live path is touched (an interruption there leaves the board wholly on the previous firmware); they then flip together with the outgoing copies kept as <dest>.bak; the deploy then IMPORTS the shipped surface on-board (fluispotter + synchronize.http + version read-back) and restores the .bak set on any failure. Plus a free-space pre-flight that aborts with the board untouched. Bench-proven both ways today: a normal deploy staged 12 files, flipped, smoked OK; then a DELIBERATELY broken version.py (the exact 2026-07-20 failure) was caught by the smoke test, 12 files rolled back, and the board came up on the previous firmware with no replug. verify 160 reqs, 139 PASS / 0 FAIL (288 tests); locks clean. 2026-07-20 a76d64f6
fw 0.1.123: #46 CAUSAL FIX — console-silent nap path + latched-sink veto (crumb 22 conviction)
Campaign 4 fired at sample 2 with breadcrumb 22: the CPU died INSIDE machine.lightsleep, watchdog fed one line earlier, crumb 23 never written. The ESP-IDF sleep-entry path flushes the console TX with interrupts OFF — and a USB-CDC host that stopped draining turns that flush into a multi-second interrupts-off window (IWDT, #108 class). The nap path was writing to that console on BOTH sides of the sleep. Fix: (a) nap path is console-silent — 'Entering'/'Woke' markers retired, crumbs 22/23 carry the identical wedge signal as RTC-memory writes with zero I/O; (b) never lightsleep while serial_sink.is_latched() — the sink's own 'host stopped draining' flag, never previously consulted. Corroboration for scope: the 44 h battery run (USB DISCONNECTED) napped thousands of times with ZERO watchdog resets vs ~1-in-13 sample-ends on every USB-attached campaign — #46 is likely a bench exposure field devices never had. SWR-274 amended (DOC 1.81); SWR-245's wake-marker test re-anchored to crumb 23. verify 159 reqs, 139 PASS / 0 FAIL. Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-07-16 bd19b90e
fw 0.1.113: safe-off moved to boot.py (earliest software moment) + AC/BP asymmetry answered (SWR-276)
Why only the AC: both DAC channels power up at zero-scale (full power in the inverted convention), but the AC enable line (GPIO 9) rests ON through reset while the BP enable (GPIO 17) rests OFF — the pumps differ ONLY in the enable line's resting state. boot.py now performs the safe-off a few hundred ms before main.py (which repeats it); the deploy set ships boot.py. The remaining pre-boot.py window (ROM + VM init, ~0.5-1 s) is closable only in hardware: pull the AC enable down so it rests OFF like the BP's. Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-07-16 e91e6843
fw 0.1.112 CRITICAL: actuator power-on safety — AC pump span at magnet-removal power-on (SWR-276)
The DAC7574 powers up at ZERO-SCALE, which is FULL POWER in this board's inverted signal convention (power = 4095 - register); with the motor-enable line floating until firmware claims it, the AC pump ran from power application until the ULP's first tick — several seconds. The old code even skipped the DAC safe-off on power-on boots on the assumption that the POR value was safe: backwards for this convention. Fix 1: main.py _actuators_safe_off() as the FIRST statements of the entry point — motor enables (AC 9, BP 17, encoders 14/18, valve 8, tape reel 41) driven LOW and DAC CH0/CH1 commanded HALT via SoftI2C within ~10 ms of software start, before USB probing or any import. Fix 2: pump_regulator.start() DAC safe-off + I2C bus recovery unconditional. Residual pre-main.py window (ROM bootloader + VM init) is hardware scope — enable-line pull-down tracked with electronics. Registry 1.67: SWR-276 + 4 structure tests (+2 stale anchors updated to the stronger unconditional behavior). 143 reqs, 125 PASS, 0 FAIL. Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
2026-07-14 ab389f19
ULP 1.12.0: cooperative AC service through BP dose/coast/park busy loops (SWR-271)
Root cause of the 8h run's residual ~3/h false AC hall warnings: BP_DOSING / BP_DOSE_COAST / BP_PARKING hold the ULP inside blocking loops for the whole dose (~10 s), coast (5 s) and park (<=30 s) — ac_tick() never runs there, so an AC hall pass falling inside such a window was LOST outright (a lost pass reads as a 2x-SPR counter gap on the CPU monitor => false 'did not advance' warning) and the AC ran open-loop on its held DAC — the owner's masking diagnosis, confirmed in source and fixed per his 'task scheduler in the ULP Dose/Park' direction. ac_busy_hall_service(): ac_tick's exact hall block (level mirror, 1->0 count, CPU wake), now shared by ac_tick and called inside every blocking BP spin (bp_drive_window, coast, park centre-offset) — no AC revolution can be lost to a BP busy window. ac_busy_regulate(): every-16th-window (~0.8 s), 150 ms- capped, 8-interval AC Simple-P slice between BP drive windows in DOSING and PARKING; its combined poll keeps counting BP encoder edges into the caller's ledger so dose/park tick accounting stays EXACT; dose-tail guard (160 edges) keeps target-crossing out of any slice; the park slice watches the BP hall so home is never overshot; no kick/ladder/stall-recovery coupling. ac_busy_slices telemetry proves regulation ran. Gates: canary linked, no libgcc, op headroom 696 B (was 1296; LOW accepted — max call depth unchanged vs the existing regulate_pump->run_simple_p chain, .stack_canary + firmware per-tick check armed, 184 B above the 512 fail line). Registry 1.64: SWR-271 + 5 structure tests; 138 reqs, 120 PASS, 0 FAIL. Deploy pending board-on-USB. Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-07-13 a053b66e
Serialize post-dose kick vs flash+WiFi (0.1.104, SWR-265)
Owner directive: 'Can we serialise the WIFI and Flash writes to prevent the reboot/WDT trigger? Do one, then the other.' Archive forensics placed BOTH 42-run WDT(code 3) resets in the post-dose window: after the last dose, _start_blood_pump() (dead-point kick, escalating-to-max power) deliberately ran CONCURRENTLY with _sync() (event-store flash commit + Wi-Fi TX) — pump-first so a hung hub cannot keep the pump parked. That rationale is kept; the sync now waits for the kick to settle first: - _await_bp_spinup(cap_ms=3000): RAM-only polling of ULP shared vars (BP_PUMP_RUNNING + per-batch BP_LAST_ELAPSED_US/PULSE_COUNT changing between polls = motor turning, kick over). No flash, no radio. On cap expiry the sync proceeds (a no-start pump is the fault path's problem). - Flush-cycle boundaries audited: RUN->REST already syncs with the pump disabled; RUN start has no sync. The dose-done site was the only kick-concurrent sync. - Also explains 'first 2h only': three BP start-FAIL escalations that morning made kicks LONG; once adaptive start settled, kicks dropped to ms and the overlap vanished (36 clean samples). 2026-07-08 c924362f
fw 0.1.92-0.1.93: verified ULP start/resume + AC-first invariant
0.1.92 — SWR-257: resume-onto-dead-ULP incident. After a power cut the RTC slow memory is random noise; the ULP start's liveness gate (INITIALIZED != 0) was satisfied by that noise and a session resumed onto a NEVER-EXECUTED ULP: no regulation, parks never serviced, encoder counts frozen (valve/tape moves gated on them misbehaved), DAC latched (runaway AC). Forensics at the REPL proved the memory was noise at the board's own address map and that a manual reload came straight up (v1.9.0, canary clear, INITIALIZED advancing). Fix: (a) ULPPumpRegulator.start zeroes INITIALIZED before run and requires it to ADVANCE (>= 2), one reload retry, then a hard failure; (b) session recovery runs the SWR-250 health verify (canary + core advancing) BEFORE any pump is enabled — on failure the session is NOT resumed and the device falls back to AWAIT_CALIBRATION with an error naming the required power-cycle. 0.1.93 — SWR-258: unanticoagulated-BP window on resume (observed twice). The recovery create_task()s the AC cycle but session entry runs synchronously — suspend no-op, instant BP kick, no-op sync — straight into the BLOCKING machine.lightsleep without one scheduler yield: the AC enable executed only at the first wake (+16 s hall / +60 s backstop). Hub log showed 'Starting AC pump cycle' at exactly entry+60 s while the BP fought its dead-point start. Fix: (a) _start_blood_pump enforces AC-FIRST at the ULP register level for state >= SESSION (SPR + preset + enable, warning event when it fires; calibration/priming states excluded by the gate — AC cycle adopts the running pump idempotently); (b) recovery yields one scheduler pass after creating the AC cycle so the enable executes before the state machine moves. Deploy tools: pump_regulator.py added to the file lists; EXPECT_VER banner brought current. Registry 1.47-1.48 (SWR-257/258, Open); 124 reqs, 109 locked, no drift; make verify 168 tests 0 fail. Both builds bench-deployed; session-reset + clean re-test in progress on 0.1.93. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>

fw 0.1.86: BP flush cycle reinstated + full lightsleep map (SWR-243..250)
The continuous in-session pumping introduced by 4cc92da was a serious battery bug (owner). Between samples the BP now runs the 2-min-run / 8-min-park flush cycle (SWR-243, LOCKED), preloads continuously at 16 SPR when a sample is <5 min out (SWR-244, LOCKED), and the flush cycle CONTINUES in FINISH so the line stays flushed (SWR-248, LOCKED). The CPU light-sleeps in every phase except dosing + fault handling (SWR-245, LOCKED; supersedes SWR-240): flush RUN and preload/loading sleep between hall ticks — the ULP wakes the CPU per hall edge to update the 7-slot deque (SWR-247, LOCKED; from Blood-Primed onward, calibration excluded) — and the 8-min REST (motor parked+off) measures ~20 mA on the bench. Sleepers are mutually exclusive by construction (flush cycle in IDLE/FINISH vs session loop in TRIGGER). Hub sync is batched to RUN boundaries + pre/post-dose + faults (SWR-246, LOCKED; supersedes per-hall-tick SWR-242 — a REST nap drops the association). Adversarial-review fixes: recovery no longer spawns the flush cycle (double-start); REST nap clamped to the preload boundary; after-REST sync skipped on the preload break (lock-hold delayed pump-start); stale _on_hall_tick early-start cancel removed (state desync). fw 0.1.78: RTC sync robustness + fault attribution + event-store hardening
0.1.77 (SWR-237): NTP outcomes are never silent — every attempt logs an event (success always, failure rate-limited), the sync retries on every command poll while the RTC is below the 2025-01-01 sanity floor, and a session transition with an insane clock raises a warning. (b) SWR-238 event-store append failures degrade to log-only instead of crashing — the 8192-entry overflow was a latent overnight session-killer at the heartbeat-spam rate. (c) SWR-239 the RTC heartbeat is edge-triggered on the revolution counter (the level-triggered %10 guard emitted ~1 event/s in ~12-event bursts since 2026-04) and reports an honest sane/UNSYNCED verdict. 2026-06-19 600454d8
ULP 1.3.3 / fw 0.1.73: adaptive self-tuning motor kick-start (SWR-126)
Every operational motor-start site now routes through ONE try_kick_start helper that learns the working start power per session — directly targeting the BP dose-completion failures (collect/prepare timeout -> fatal reset). Algorithm (uniform at every start site): drive at the pump's learned start register; wait <=50 ms for the first encoder edge (sample_encoder's first-edge window). Edge -> started, keep the power. No edge -> set MOTOR_START_FAIL + wake CPU (logged), stop the motor, escalate start power +200 (register -= 200, MORE power, clamped to full DAC_MAX_POWER), persist the new register in the shared var, retry next tick. One attempt per tick + clamped register => no infinite loop. The escalated value persists for the session and resets to the board_config default at boot — so it ideally escalates only once per session. Start sites unified (esp32/pump_regulator/main/ulp/main.c): regulate_pump spin-up (AC + BP running) + mid-run stall recovery; bp_enter_dosing / bp_enter_parking via bp_dose_park_kick; BP_DOSING + BP_PARKING mid-stall (replaced the one-shot nudge-to-DAC_START). New per-pump shared vars ac_dac_start / bp_dac_start. Config: board_config DAC_START_AC = 700 power (reg 3395), DAC_START_BP = 3200 power (reg 895, strong stiction-breaking) — both overlay-tunable; firmware converts power->register (4095 - power) at ULP init and logs "BP/AC start power raised to <pwr> (kick reg=<reg>)" on each escalation. POWER/REGISTER INVERSION verified correct: power = 4095 - register; escalate lowers the register (more power); clamp [DAC_MAX_POWER=0 .. DAC_HALT-POWER_FLOOR]. Verified off-device: build green — operational 6480 B, headroom +1416 B, no libgcc, ULP 1.3.3; test/test2 fit. variables.py/test_variables/test2_variables regenerated (AC_DAC_START/BP_DAC_START present, no hardcoded hex). SWR-126 added (+ SWR-111/113 reworded), doc 1.13 (91 reqs); SW suite 66 PASS / 0 FAIL; lint clean. NOT deployed, NOT bench-tested — operational binary intentionally changed (build_verify "byte-identical" gate re-passes after deploy). NOT YET DONE (user-driven, needs the board): deploy the rebuilt operational ULP (ulp_main.bin) + fw 0.1.73, RESTART the Control app (AC/BP_DAC_START addresses shifted), then bench-test via the Dose/Park Debug tab — each Dose/Park click now exercises the adaptive kick-start. Watch for "start power raised" + that the learned value persists for the session. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-06-18 fefc581d
firmware: session resilience + crash-boot DAC recovery + diagnostics
Session resilience (overnight-stall fix): - ntp_synchronize is best-effort (returns False, never raises) so a Wi-Fi/NTP timeout can no longer crash the firmware — the overnight AWAIT_CALIBRATION ETIMEDOUT crash-loop. Recovery Wi-Fi/sync is wrapped so an out-of-range radio is skipped and the session resumes offline. - _state_session persists state=SESSION + sample index via _write_state on entry and on each sample advance, so a mid-session reset recovers and continues the schedule (state.dat previously froze at AWAIT_CALIBRATION, so recovery was dead code). Bench-confirmed: state.dat held state=10 after a mid-session reset. - log_error appends + timestamps (16 KB cap) so the first fault survives a reset loop. Crash-boot DAC7574 recovery (A+B gated by reset_cause): - ULPPumpRegulator.start(), on any non-power-on reset, runs an I2C SCL clock-out bus recovery then commands DAC7574 CH0/CH1 (pump signals) OFF before the RTC takes the bus — clears a crash-latched DAC / stuck bus without a power-cycle. Best-effort; skipped on a clean power-on. Diagnostics: - Motor-stop events logged decoded (per-pump stall vs never-started) with the sample index + each pump's DAC/power. main_debug.py: fix stale `Controller` import so the debug build runs again. Verification: SWR-230/231/232/233 + regression tests; requirements doc v1.9 (86 requirements); SW suite green. Deployed + bench-verified on fw 0.1.70. 2026-06-17 d82e61d5
fix(ci): let ULP-build generate the tree, link via build_verify_ulp.py
The ulp-build job failed at `idf.py build` with `undefined reference to ulp_stack_canary`: main.c declares that stack-overflow sentinel extern, and it's defined only by the version-controlled esp32/pump_regulator/main/ulp/ulp_riscv.ld. But idf.py regenerates ulp_riscv.ld from the stock ESP-IDF template (no canary), so the ULP link fails. The bench never hits this because build_verify_ulp.py copies our ulp_riscv.ld over the generated one and re-links — but CI ran raw `idf.py build` and aborted (sh -e) before that step. Fix: `idf.py build || true` — it only needs to generate the ULP ninja tree (it reaches the final ULP link before failing, so the tree is complete). build_verify_ulp.py then installs main/ulp/ulp_riscv.ld and re-links/objcopies all three images + runs the size/headroom/libgcc/ version gates. A genuine earlier build break still fails the job (ninja has no tree to run against). Verified in CI (no local ESP-IDF toolchain). Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>
2026-06-17 9ebdd2e9
fix(ci): drop dead `import serial` from output/_serial_io.py
The SW verification CI job failed with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'serial'`: test_board_config_overlay imports `output._serial_io.fnv`, and _serial_io.py imported pyserial at module top — but CI doesn't install pyserial. 2026-06-10 777acee9
ULP Simple-P: count HALL revs through the skip-ladder idle
test_p_regulate() sampled the hall switch only inside the N=16 measurement loop. While idling on the skip ladder it just spun on rtc_ticks() without reading hall, so a revolution completing during the idle (up to 1.58 s at skip level 5) was missed — under-counting both the revolution total and the `revs` run-length stop. Fix: - ULP (main.c): the skip-idle loop now polls the hall switch every iteration, counts revolutions continuously, and honors the rev-count stop during the idle. Revolution tracking is now independent of whether the regulation measurement is skipped. - Host (monitor/board_script.py): pid_p_regulate_script detected the rev boundary only on a measurement update (test_rev_done change); rev detection now runs every poll so revolutions counted during a long idle surface live. test_p_regulate is shared by the test + test2 images, so both versions bump: ULP_Test2 1.3.0 -> 1.3.1, ULP_Test 1.2.13 -> 1.2.14. Operational stays byte-identical (1.2.4). test2 = 3204 B (under the 4592 B RTC-load ceiling, 4772 B headroom), no new libgcc. test2_variables.py regenerated for the shifted addresses. Bench-validated on AC (Kp=7, skip_max=5): converged, climbed to skip level 5, and counted all revolutions through the 1.63 s sparse sampling (hall_rev 0->4, ~16 s apart); measured per-level sampling rate matched theory. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>

[screenshot N=32 kp3000 ki360000] Still an issue with N implementation into PID algo. Found TWO residual N-deps in 1.2.10: (a) ERR_CLAMP applied to the RAW error BEFORE normalization → usable normalized range = ERR_CLAMP·TPC/N, so at large N moderate/large slowdowns were throttled; (b) integral ran on the RAW error (∝N) → 16× larger steps at N=32 → windup/oscillation. N=16 makes regulation go crazy — N not handled correctly in the PID. Root cause: error = measured_ticks − target_ticks scales with the window (∝ N), and P/D multiply that raw error, so loop gain ∝ N (N=16 ≈ 8× the N=2 tuning → oscillation). Fix (ULP_Test 1.2.10, test_pid_regulate only): P/D use a window-normalized error e_norm = error*TARGET_PULSE_COUNT/pulse_count (udiv64, sign-handled); INTEGRAL keeps the raw error (raw ≈ ∫err·dt, wall-clock rate already N-independent); deadband compares e_norm; pid_tune_error stays raw (host _tgt=_meas-_err). Loop now invariant to N; at N=TARGET_PULSE_COUNT(=2) identical to before so current gains hold. Operational run_pid untouched → bin byte-identical (1.2.4). Off-device verified (op byte-identical, test 4584B, headroom 3108B, only __ashldi3). PENDING DEPLOY (drives BP motor; close Control app). run_pid needs the same split when promoting.

"In control 'Pumps' The BP controls do not start the SimpleP correctly." Root cause (code-verified): the Pumps tab was built for the legacy positional kp/ki/kd PID and never arms the operational Simple-P — it wrote KP/KI/KD_SCALED + target + dac + run, with NO step_per_pct/op_p_ and no reseed. BP-SPECIFIC blocker: the ULP BP state machine has BP_DOSING and BP_PARKING states that PRE-EMPT BP_RUNNING (higher priority), so bp_pump_running=1 alone may never reach BP_RUNNING (→ Simple-P never engages). AC has no such states, which is why AC worked and BP didn't. Fix (pump_panel.py): added a _arm_preamble() mirroring the proven PID-Tuner Operational arming — clears bp_dosing/bp_parking (BP only, via pre_clear=(BP_DOSING,BP_PARKING)), stops the pump + sleep 150ms for a clean IDLE→RUNNING reseed, writes the per-pump step_per_pct (AC 7 / BP 3) + op_p_ bounds (25/64/4095/6/4) + clears motor_stop_events; then target + DAC + run=1. Power mode now writes step=0 (Simple-P holds the seeded power). Apply re-asserts step + target. Addresses getattr-guarded from variables.py (degrades on older firmware). Verified: compile OK; replicated command generation shows BP clears dosing/parking + reseed + step=3 + op_p_* + target, AC has no dosing clear + step=7. NEEDS HARDWARE TEST (host-side change; can't bench-test motor here). Control bumped 1.1.5→1.1.6. Uncommitted.

The Pumps tab preset DAC gave start_power=1000 — too low for BP. Sequence: BP_IDLE→RUNNING → at power 1000 BP doesn't sustain → count==0 → spin-up KICK (DAC_START → escalates to DAC_MAX_POWER, the ~4000 spikes) → spins → first run_simple_p re-seeds pwr=1000 → stalls again → re-kick → limit cycle (runs fast ~11 s/rev on the kick-heavy average). AC sustains at low power (settles 349) so AC is fine at 1000. The validated clean BP@3 used the PID Tuner with start_power=3200. "The verification metric for the ULP SimpleP regulation is to base the SPR calculation on TIME BETWEEN HALL, not Encoder sampling time interval." hall-interval is the TRUE rev time. RATIONALE: the encoder batch (elapsed_us*5184/pulses) is a 1/324-rev sub-sample dominated by within-rev peristaltic ripple — it bounces ±10% sample-to-sample (BP read ~14.x mid-rev, only the final partial batch read 16.5; THAT single value was what triggered the earlier 17.3-vs-16 false fail). Board-side time between consecutive hall_revolutions increments = a full true revolution, which averages the ripple out. CHANGE: output/run_operational_convergence.py drive_script now tracks last-hall _lh + hall-time _ht, computes sprh=ticks_diff(now,_ht)/1000/dh on each hall increment, emits sprh in the op frame (25 ms poll for edge resolution); main() summary reports sprh_mean (mean of hall intervals, first/spin-up discarded). verification/tests_mech/test_bench.py grades sprh_mean (not the encoder-batch tail-mean) against ±5%, requiring ≥3 hall intervals + stop==0 + hall>0. SWR-122/123 text now SPECIFIES the hall-interval metric; registry DOC_VERSION 1.1→1.2; docs/fluispotter_sw_requirements.html+.docx regenerated. HARDWARE (device on /dev/cu.usbmodem101, apps closed, pyserial RawRepl): AC @20: sprh_mean=19.808 (−0.96%), 3 hall-intervals 19.78/19.83/19.83, pwr 421, skip 6, stop 0. BP @16: sprh_mean=16.000 (0.00%), 4 hall-intervals 16.06/15.97/16.04/15.94, pwr ~2700, skip→6, stop 0 — vs encoder-batch bouncing 14.31–16.57 over the same run (the metric's whole point). Formal MECH suite: SWR-122/123/124/125 ALL PASS (4/4). Pumps reset idle, stop=0. "In Control 'Sensors' add a sparkline showing the status of the ON/Switch (Hall element). Give an overview of how it works and verify it is correctly set up in sw." added a digital ON/OFF sparkline for the GPIO5 reed/Hall power-ON switch. WHAT IT IS: spec §4.8 "Hall / Reed ON Switch Mechanism" — GPIO5 is the device power-ON trigger. Rev A–D: Standex-Meder MK24 reed (SPST NC); Rev E: TI TMAG5231B1 Hall (drop-in, same polarity). Packaging magnet holds it CLOSED → GPIO5 LOW = hard power-OFF; remove from packaging → opens → PULL_UP wins → GPIO5 HIGH = powered ON. (Dev bypass jumper available.) CHANGE (host-side only — Control app; board_script is injected via raw REPL, NOT deployed to the board): - monitor/board_script.py: sensors_monitor_script() (the Sensors-tab stream) now reads _p_onsw = Pin(5, IN, PULL_UP) and emits 'on_switch': _p_onsw.value(); MONITOR_SCRIPT also emits 'on_switch' (alias of existing _p_reed GPIO5) for cross-tab continuity. - control/panels/sensors_panel.py: _Sparkline gains a binary mode (fixed 0/1 rail, steps-post trace, ON/OFF readout coloured green/dim); new _ROWS entry 'on_switch' (top row). update()/repopulate() feed it generically. - control/app_version.py 1.1.9 → 1.1.10. "And Brown-Out-Detect?" / "Is there any reason NOT to enable BOD?" / "You say WDT but I dont think we enabled the WDT — it collided with the lightsleep and tripped just because we were sleeping." / "I dont think we enabled BOD." / "Note this: WIFI and EEPROM writes are busy tasks that will bind the CPU. Are we sure a log write and a WIFI comms cannot collide? We introduced an interrupt to wake the CPU from lightsleep — what happens if that interrupt hits while we are both writing and WIFI updating?" register read RTC_CNTL_BROWN_OUT_REG 0x600080D4 = 0x02719000: BOD ENA/RST_ENA = 0 → confirmed DISABLED. Enabling BOD is orthogonal safety (no downside for a mains/USB-powered medical device); it also converts any real sag to a clean brownout reset instead of a silent glitch. Sten's WIFI+EEPROM-bind-the-CPU is the ground truth (memory wifi_flash_cpu_binding); the wake-interrupt-during-flash-write hazard is the collision class the IWDT margin protects against. Mechanism guidance accepted.

141. 2026-07-16 — CRITICAL: "AC pump starts spinning immediately" on magnet removal (SWR-276, fw 0.1.112)

·
> Root cause: DAC7574 POR = zero-scale = FULL POWER in the inverted convention (power = 4095 -
·
register) + motor enable floating until firmware claims it -> AC ran from power application until
·
the ULP's first tick (seconds). Pre-existing power-on gap (nothing in 0.1.111 touches it) — first
·
observed because the magnet test put eyes on the pump at power-on; the old code even SKIPPED the
·
DAC safe-off on PWRON boots ("POR already cleared it" — backwards). Fix: main.py
·
_actuators_safe_off() as the FIRST statements (enables 9/17/14/18/8/41 LOW + DAC CH0/CH1 HALT via
·
SoftI2C, ~10 ms in); pump_regulator.start() safe-off now UNCONDITIONAL. Residual pre-main.py window
·
(ROM bootloader + VM init ~0.5-1.5 s) = HARDWARE scope: enable-line pull-down (electronics/QCR).

153. 2026-07-17 — "Hub only partially updates automatically... stale ONLINE status visible."

·
> ROOT CAUSE: the ONLINE/OFFLINE badge renders from access_points.connected, which is written
·
by /usr/bin/synchronized-hostapd-handler — a SHELL SCRIPT hostapd_cli spawns on (dis)associate,
·
writing sqlite DIRECTLY. The service process never observes that mutation, so it never SSE-
·
broadcasts: DB correct, open pages stale (ONLINE stuck until manual refresh; ONLINE-side often
·
looked live only because the device's first sync POST triggered its own broadcast). FIX hub
·
1.7.3 (fef85a6, LIVE): _connectivity_watch background task in http/server.py polls the hostapd
·
station list every 8 s, set-diffs MACs, looks up joined/left devices (devices.mac_address,
·
6-byte bytes) and sse.broadcast()s each — OFFLINE now propagates to open pages within ~8 s,
·
no page code changed. Verified live: board napping mid-session, station list without its MAC,
·
page badge OFFLINE on v1.7.3 footer; the running session's next wake/nap flip is the natural
·
live proof on Sten's open page.

171. 2026-07-18 — "verify that the deque is updated EVERY TIME the BP passes the Hall"

·
> VERIFIED: NO — bp_hall_revolutions++ exists at exactly ONE site (main.c:1997, case BP_RUNNING
·
only). BP_DOSING/BP_PARKING read the hall solely as a stop condition (bp_drive_window
·
watch_hall/hall_found; parking arrival) — crossings there are DETECTED but never counted, never
·
woken. Each dose cycle crosses the hall once (during park) -> tube advances one segment
·
invisibly to the ledger. CPU deque (7 entries) counts deltas losslessly but batches timestamps
·
(N revs seen in one poll share one stamp — minor skew). Doses 2..N start BLIND (no deque
·
consult). Impact bounded honestly: dose 1 of well-separated samples always correct (gate window
·
contains only RUNNING revs — why all audits were clean); intra-set doses hit non-adjacent
·
segments + the full-rev delay; 2-min tables marginal (park crossing can fall in the next gate
·
window). Plan v2: ULP counts crossings in EVERY state (SWR-289) + intra-set gate on ring
·
advance (SWR-288).
